Mathlib.Data.Nat.Factorization

Statements of Mathlib (commit a88727a581), each with its Lean source, its translation to Dedukti by Export.lean, and its informalization by Informath, in English, as a formula, and in French, Czech, German and Swedish, which the buttons at each entry show there, and the check boxes above the entries everywhere. The Dedukti translation drops implicit and instance arguments and maps Lean's arithmetic and logic to Informath's base (mapping.tsv); the other Mathlib constants are worded by the symbol table mathlib.dkgf, whose English wordings Informath also renders in the other languages. Constants without a wording appear as $\mathrm{name}$.

Nat.factorizationdefDefs.lean:51
n.factorization is the finitely supported function ℕ →₀ ℕ mapping each prime factor of n to its multiplicity in n.
Lean
def factorization (n : ℕ) : ℕ →₀ ℕ :=
  Multiset.toFinsupp n.primeFactorsList
Dedukti
2 lines
def Nat__factorization : (n : Elem Nat) -> Elem (Finsupp Nat Nat) :=
  n => Multiset__toFinsupp__app (Multiset__ofList (Nat__primeFactorsList n)).

Let $n \in \Natural$. Then the factorization of $n$ is an element of $\FinsuppMACRoBB {\Natural}{\Natural}$ defined as $\operatorname{toFinsupp}$ applied to the multiset of the prime factorization of $n$.

Every nonzero natural number has a unique prime factorization

  eq_of_factorization_eq ha hb fun p => by simp [h]

Nat.«termOrdProj[_]_»defDefs.lean:322
We introduce the notations ordProj[p] n for the largest power of the prime p that divides n and ordCompl[p] n for the complementary part. The ord naming comes from the $p$-adic order/valuation of a number, and proj and compl are for the projection and complementary projection. The term n.factorization p is the $p$-adic order itself. For example, ordProj[2] n is the even part of n and ordCompl[2] n is the odd part.
Lean
notation "ordProj[" p "] " n:arg => p ^ Nat.factorization n p

Nat.factorizationLCMLeftdefDefs.lean:352
If a = ∏ pᵢ ^ nᵢ and b = ∏ pᵢ ^ mᵢ, then factorizationLCMLeft = ∏ pᵢ ^ kᵢ, where kᵢ = nᵢ if mᵢ ≤ nᵢ and 0 otherwise. Note that the product is over the divisors of lcm a b, so if one of a or b is 0 then the result is 1.
Lean
def factorizationLCMLeft (a b : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  (Nat.lcm a b).factorization.prod fun p n ↦
    if b.factorization p ≤ a.factorization p then p ^ n else 1

Nat.factorizationLCMRightdefDefs.lean:365
If a = ∏ pᵢ ^ nᵢ and b = ∏ pᵢ ^ mᵢ, then factorizationLCMRight = ∏ pᵢ ^ kᵢ, where kᵢ = mᵢ if nᵢ < mᵢ and 0 otherwise. Note that the product is over the divisors of lcm a b, so if one of a or b is 0 then the result is 1.
Note that factorizationLCMRight a b is not factorizationLCMLeft b a: the difference is that in factorizationLCMLeft a b there are the primes whose exponent in a is bigger or equal than the exponent in b, while in factorizationLCMRight a b there are the primes whose exponent in b is strictly bigger than in a. For example factorizationLCMLeft 2 2 = 2, but factorizationLCMRight 2 2 = 1.
Lean
def factorizationLCMRight (a b : ℕ) :=
  (Nat.lcm a b).factorization.prod fun p n ↦
    if b.factorization p ≤ a.factorization p then 1 else p ^ n
Dedukti
2 lines
def Nat__factorizationLCMRight : (a : Elem Nat) -> (b : Elem Nat) -> Elem Nat :=
  a => b => Finsupp__prod (Nat__factorization (Nat__lcm a b)) (p => n => ite (Leq (Nat__factorization__app b p) (Nat__factorization__app a p)) 1 (pow p n)).

Nat.factorization_eq_card_pow_dvd_of_lttheoremBasic.lean:434
The factorization of m in n is the number of positive natural numbers i such that m ^ i divides n. Note m is prime. This set is expressed by filtering Ico 1 b where b is any bound greater than log m n.

Nat.recOnPrimePowdefInduction.lean:27
Given P 0, P 1 and a way to extend P a to P (p ^ n * a) for prime p not dividing a, we can define P for all natural numbers.
Lean
@[elab_as_elim]
def recOnPrimePow {motive : ℕ → Sort*} (zero : motive 0) (one : motive 1)
    (prime_pow_mul : ∀ a p n : ℕ, p.Prime → ¬p ∣ a → 0 < n → motive a → motive (p ^ n * a)) :
    ∀ a, motive a :=
Dedukti
1 lines
Nat__recOnPrimePow : (zero : Elem (motive 0)) -> (one : Elem (motive 1)) -> (prime_pow_mul : ((a1 : Elem Nat) -> (p : Elem Nat) -> (n : Elem Nat) -> Proof (prime p) -> Proof (not (divide p a1)) -> Proof (Lt 0 n) -> Elem (motive a1) -> motive (times (pow p n) a1))) -> (a : Elem Nat) -> Elem (motive a).
English
Let $\mathrm{zero}$ be an element of $\mathrm{motive}$ applied to $0$. Let $\mathrm{one}$ be an element of $\mathrm{motive}$ applied to $1$. Let $\mathrm{prime\_pow\_mul}$ be a function from natural numbers $a_{1}$ and natural numbers $p$ and natural numbers $n$ and elements of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to the proposition that $p$ is prime and elements of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to the proposition that $p$ does not divide $a_{1}$ and elements of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to $\mathrm{Lt} (0 , n)$ and elements of $\mathrm{motive}$ applied to $a_{1}$ to elements of $\mathrm{motive}$ applied to $p ^ {n}\times a_{1}$. Let $a \in \Natural$. Then $\operatorname{recOnPrimePow}$ applied to $\mathrm{zero}$, $\mathrm{one}$, $a_{1}$, $p$, $n$, $\mathrm{prime\_pow\_mul}$ and $a$ is an element of $\mathrm{motive}$ applied to $a$.
French
Soit $\mathrm{zero}$ un élément de $\mathrm{motive}$ appliqué à $0$. Soit $\mathrm{one}$ un élément de $\mathrm{motive}$ appliqué à $1$. Soit $\mathrm{prime\_pow\_mul}$ une fonction des nombres naturels $a_{1}$ et des nombres naturels $p$ et des nombres naturels $n$ et des éléments de $\mathrm{Proof}$ appliqué à la proposition que $p$ est premier et des éléments de $\mathrm{Proof}$ appliqué à la proposition que $p$ ne divise pas $a_{1}$ et des éléments de $\mathrm{Proof}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{Lt} (0 , n)$ et des éléments de $\mathrm{motive}$ appliqué à $a_{1}$ à des éléments de $\mathrm{motive}$ appliqué à $p ^ {n}\times a_{1}$. Soit $a \in \Natural$. Alors $\operatorname{recOnPrimePow}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{zero}$, $\mathrm{one}$, $a_{1}$, $p$, $n$, $\mathrm{prime\_pow\_mul}$ et $a$ est un élément de $\mathrm{motive}$ appliqué à $a$.
Czech
Nechť $\mathrm{zero}$ je prvek $\mathrm{motive}$ aplikovaný na $0$. Nechť $\mathrm{one}$ je prvek $\mathrm{motive}$ aplikovaný na $1$. Nechť $\mathrm{prime\_pow\_mul}$ je funkce z přirozených čísl $a_{1}$ a přirozených čísl $p$ a přirozených čísl $n$ a prvků $\mathrm{Proof}$ aplikovaný na výrok že $p$ je prvočíselné a prvků $\mathrm{Proof}$ aplikovaný na výrok že $p$ nedělí $a_{1}$ a prvků $\mathrm{Proof}$ aplikovaný na $\mathrm{Lt} (0 , n)$ a prvků $\mathrm{motive}$ aplikovaný na $a_{1}$ do prvků $\mathrm{motive}$ aplikovaný na $p ^ {n}\times a_{1}$. Nechť $a \in \Natural$. Tedy $\operatorname{recOnPrimePow}$ aplikovaný na $\mathrm{zero}$, $\mathrm{one}$, $a_{1}$, $p$, $n$, $\mathrm{prime\_pow\_mul}$ a $a$ je prvek $\mathrm{motive}$ aplikovaný na $a$.
German
Sei $\mathrm{zero}$ ein Element von $\mathrm{motive}$ angewandt auf $0$. Sei $\mathrm{one}$ ein Element von $\mathrm{motive}$ angewandt auf $1$. Sei $\mathrm{prime\_pow\_mul}$ eine Funktion aus natürlichen Zahlen $a_{1}$ und natürlichen Zahlen $p$ und natürlichen Zahlen $n$ und Elementen von $\mathrm{Proof}$ angewandt auf den Satz, dass $p$ prim ist, und Elementen von $\mathrm{Proof}$ angewandt auf den Satz, dass $p$ nicht $a_{1}$ teilt, und Elementen von $\mathrm{Proof}$ angewandt auf $\mathrm{Lt} (0 , n)$ und Elementen von $\mathrm{motive}$ angewandt auf $a_{1}$ nach Elementen von $\mathrm{motive}$ angewandt auf $p ^ {n}\times a_{1}$. Nat.recOnPosPrimePosCoprimedefInduction.lean:49
Given P 0, P 1, and P (p ^ n) for positive prime powers, and a way to extend P a and P b to P (a * b) when a, b are positive coprime, we can define P for all natural numbers.
Lean
@[elab_as_elim]
def recOnPosPrimePosCoprime {motive : ℕ → Sort*}
    (prime_pow : ∀ p n : ℕ, Prime p → 0 < n → motive (p ^ n))
    (zero : motive 0) (one : motive 1)
    (coprime : ∀ a b, 1 < a → 1 < b → Coprime a b → motive a → motive b → motive (a * b)) :
    ∀ a, motive a :=
  recOnPrimePow zero one <| by
    intro a p n hp' hpa hn hPa
    by_cases ha1 : a = 1
    · rw [ha1, mul_one]
      exact prime_pow p n hp' hn
    refine coprime (p ^ n) a (hp'.one_lt.trans_le (le_self_pow hn.ne' _)) ?_ ?_
      (prime_pow _ _ hp' hn) hPa
    · contrapose! hpa
      simp [lt_one_iff.1 (lt_of_le_of_ne hpa ha1)]
    · simpa [hn, Prime.coprime_iff_not_dvd hp']

@[elab_as_elim]
def recOnPrimeCoprime {motive : ℕ → Sort*} (zero : motive 0)
    (prime_pow : ∀ p n : ℕ, Prime p → motive (p ^ n))
    (coprime : ∀ a b, 1 < a → 1 < b → Coprime a b → motive a → motive b → motive (a * b)) :
    ∀ a, motive a :=
  recOnPosPrimePosCoprime (fun p n h _ => prime_pow p n h) zero (prime_pow 2 0 prime_two) coprime

Nat.Primes.prodNatEquivdefPrimePow.lean:151
The canonical equivalence between pairs (p, k) with p a prime and k : ℕ and the set of prime powers given by (p, k) ↦ p^(k+1).
Lean
def Nat.Primes.prodNatEquiv : Nat.Primes × ℕ ≃ {n : ℕ // IsPrimePow n} where
  toFun pk :=
    ⟨pk.1 ^ (pk.2 + 1), ⟨pk.1, pk.2 + 1, prime_iff.mp pk.1.prop, pk.2.add_one_pos, rfl⟩⟩
  invFun n :=
    (⟨n.val.minFac, minFac_prime n.prop.ne_one⟩, n.val.factorization n.val.minFac - 1)
  left_inv := fun (p, k) ↦ by
    simp only [p.prop.pow_minFac k.add_one_ne_zero, Subtype.coe_eta, factorization_pow, p.prop,
      Prime.factorization, Finsupp.smul_single, smul_eq_mul, mul_one, Finsupp.single_add,
      Finsupp.coe_add, Pi.add_apply, Finsupp.single_eq_same, add_tsub_cancel_right]
  right_inv n := by
    ext1
    dsimp only
    rw [sub_one_add_one (Nat.factorization_minFac_ne_zero n.prop.one_lt),
      n.prop.minFac_pow_factorization_eq]

2 lines

Mathlib.Data.Nat.Factorization.Root source

Nat.floorRootdefRoot.lean:53
Flooring root of a natural number. This divides the valuation of every prime number rounding down.
Eg if n = 2, a = 2^3 * 3^2 * 5, then floorRoot n a = 2 * 3.
In order theory terms, this is the upper or right adjoint of the map a ↦ a ^ n : ℕ → ℕ where ℕ is ordered by divisibility.
To ensure that the adjunction (Nat.pow_dvd_iff_dvd_floorRoot) holds in as many cases as possible, we special-case the following values: * floorRoot 0 a = 0 * floorRoot n 0 = 0
Lean
def floorRoot (n a : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  if n = 0 ∨ a = 0 then 0 else a.factorization.prod fun p k ↦ p ^ (k / n)
Dedukti
2 lines

Nat.ceilRootdefRoot.lean:112
Ceiling root of a natural number. This divides the valuation of every prime number rounding up.
Eg if n = 3, a = 2^4 * 3^2 * 5, then ceilRoot n a = 2^2 * 3 * 5.
In order theory terms, this is the lower or left adjoint of the map a ↦ a ^ n : ℕ → ℕ where ℕ is ordered by divisibility.
To ensure that the adjunction (Nat.dvd_pow_iff_ceilRoot_dvd) holds in as many cases as possible, we special-case the following values: * ceilRoot 0 a = 0 (this one is not strictly necessary) * ceilRoot n 0 = 0
Lean
def ceilRoot (n a : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  if n = 0 ∨ a = 0 then 0 else a.factorization.prod fun p k ↦ p ^ ((k + n - 1) / n)

Nat.dvd_pow_iff_ceilRoot_dvdtheoremRoot.lean:153
Galois connection between ceilRoot n : ℕ → ℕ and a ↦ a ^ n : ℕ → ℕ where ℕ is ordered by divisibility.
Note that this cannot possibly hold for n = 0, regardless of the value of ceilRoot 0 a, because the statement reduces to a = 1 ↔ ceilRoot 0 a ∣ b, which is false for e.g. a = 0, b = ceilRoot 0 a.
